<a id='question-051'></a>

### Question 051: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 01 (expression)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the molecular mechanisms underlying B and T cell activation, specifically focusing on the role of costimulatory molecules (like CD28/B7 interaction) and the downstream signaling pathways (like PI3K/Akt) required for full lymphocyte activation, proliferation, and cytokine production, distinguishing it from anergy or tolerance. (理解B和T细胞激活的分子机制，特别是共刺激分子（如CD28/B7相互作用）和下游信号通路（如PI3K/Akt）在细胞完全激活、增殖和细胞因子产生中的作用，并将其与无能或耐受区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, myalgia, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. He recently returned from a 2-week trip to Southeast Asia where he was bitten multiple times by mosquitoes. He denies any known allergies or chronic medical conditions. Physical examination reveals a temperature of 38.5°C, tachycardia, and a widespread rash predominantly on his trunk and extremities. Laboratory tests show leukopenia and thrombocytopenia. A skin biopsy from the rash reveals endothelial cell swelling and fibrin deposition in small vessels, consistent with vasculitis. Serological testing is positive for IgM antibodies against Dengue virus, but negative for IgG antibodies. Flow cytometry analysis of peripheral blood lymphocytes shows decreased expression of CD28 on T cells and decreased expression of B7-1 (CD80) and B7-2 (CD86) on antigen-presenting cells (APCs). The patient's T cells fail to proliferate in response to stimulation with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 antibodies in vitro, but do proliferate when stimulated with anti-CD3 alone in the presence of high concentrations of IL-2.

Which of the following molecular pathways is most likely impaired in this patient, leading to the observed T cell hyporesponsiveness?

(A) Activation of the NF-κB pathway downstream of the TCR complex.
(B) Phosphorylation of the ZAP-70 kinase following TCR engagement.
(C) Recruitment and activation of the PI3K/Akt pathway via CD28 costimulation.
(D) Upregulation of PD-1 expression on T cells leading to inhibitory signaling.
(E) Formation of the immunological synapse between the T cell and APC.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** Young adult male with fever, rash, myalgia after travel to Southeast Asia with mosquito bites. This suggests a mosquito-borne viral illness, likely Dengue fever given the IgM positivity and clinical picture.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Leukopenia and thrombocytopenia are characteristic of Dengue fever. Vasculitis on skin biopsy is also consistent.
- **Immunological Findings:** The crucial clue is the decreased expression of CD28 on T cells and B7-1/B7-2 on APCs. This points towards a defect in costimulation.
- **Functional Assay:** T cells fail to proliferate with anti-CD3 + anti-CD28 stimulation but *do* proliferate with anti-CD3 + high-dose IL-2. This indicates that the primary T cell receptor (TCR) signaling pathway (initiated by anti-CD3) is intact, but the costimulatory signal (normally provided by anti-CD28 engaging B7) is deficient. The rescue by high-dose IL-2 bypasses the need for CD28 costimulation, as IL-2 signaling activates the PI3K/Akt pathway, which is downstream of CD28.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the impaired molecular pathway responsible for the T cell hyporesponsiveness.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Activation:** Full T cell activation requires two signals:
    1.  **Signal 1:** TCR engagement by peptide-MHC complex on APCs. This activates intracellular signaling cascades involving kinases like Lck, ZAP-70, and ultimately leads to activation of transcription factors like NF-κB and AP-1.
    2.  **Signal 2 (Costimulation):** Interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on APCs. This signal is crucial for preventing anergy and promoting full T cell activation, proliferation, and cytokine production.
- **CD28/B7 Signaling:** Binding of CD28 to B7 recruits and activates phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase (PI3K). PI3K phosphorylates PIP2 to PIP3, which recruits Akt (also known as protein kinase B, PKB) and other downstream signaling molecules. The PI3K/Akt pathway is essential for T cell survival, proliferation, and cytokine production (especially IL-2 production).
- **Anergy:** If T cells receive Signal 1 (TCR engagement) but lack Signal 2 (costimulation), they become anergic (unresponsive) or undergo apoptosis. This is a mechanism of peripheral tolerance.
- **Dengue Virus and Immune Evasion:** Dengue virus infection can lead to immune dysregulation, including T cell exhaustion and hyporesponsiveness. While the exact mechanisms are complex and not fully elucidated, viral proteins or the host's inflammatory response can interfere with costimulatory molecule expression or signaling. The vignette describes a scenario mimicking costimulation deficiency (decreased CD28/B7 expression) leading to T cell anergy, which can be observed in certain viral infections.
- **Rescue by IL-2:** IL-2 signaling also activates the PI3K/Akt pathway, bypassing the need for CD28 costimulation. This explains why adding high-dose IL-2 can rescue T cell proliferation in the in vitro assay.
- **Therefore:** The impaired pathway is the one initiated by CD28 costimulation, which involves the recruitment and activation of PI3K/Akt.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Activation of the NF-κB pathway downstream of the TCR complex (Signal 1). This pathway is initiated by TCR engagement (Signal 1) and is *not* dependent on CD28 costimulation. The vignette shows that TCR signaling (Signal 1) is intact, as evidenced by the rescue with IL-2, which bypasses CD28. Therefore, NF-κB activation is likely occurring upon TCR stimulation, even if it's insufficient for full activation without costimulation.
- (B): Phosphorylation of the ZAP-70 kinase following TCR engagement. ZAP-70 is a key kinase in the TCR signaling pathway (Signal 1), downstream of the TCR complex and upstream of NF-κB and other pathways. Like NF-κB, ZAP-70 activation is part of Signal 1 and is not dependent on CD28 costimulation. The rescue by IL-2 implies Signal 1 is functional.
- (D): Upregulation of PD-1 expression on T cells leading to inhibitory signaling. PD-1 is an inhibitory receptor expressed on exhausted T cells. While T cell exhaustion can occur in chronic infections and lead to hyporesponsiveness, the primary defect described in the vignette is the *lack* of costimulation (decreased CD28/B7), not the presence of an inhibitory signal like PD-1. Furthermore, the rescue by IL-2 is more characteristic of costimulation deficiency than PD-1 blockade.
- (E): Formation of the immunological synapse between the T cell and APC. The immunological synapse is the specialized junction formed between a T cell and an APC, facilitating signal exchange. While costimulatory molecules (like CD28 and B7) are concentrated within the synapse, the *formation* of the synapse itself is not the primary defect described. The defect is the *lack of functional costimulation* within that synapse due to decreased expression of CD28 and B7. The vignette implies the synapse can form (TCR engagement occurs), but the necessary costimulatory signal is missing.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (C) - PI3K/Akt Pathway (CD28 Costimulation) | (A) NF-κB Pathway (Signal 1) | (B) ZAP-70 Phosphorylation (Signal 1) | (D) PD-1 Signaling (Inhibition) | (E) Immunological Synapse Formation |
| :---------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------- | :--------------------------- | :----------------------------------- | :----------------------------- | :----------------------------------- |
| **Dependency on CD28**   | Directly dependent                                         | Independent                  | Independent                          | Independent                    | Indirectly related (molecules involved) |
| **Role in T Cell Activation** | Essential for full activation, proliferation, survival     | Essential for activation     | Essential for activation              | Inhibitory                     | Facilitates signal exchange          |
| **Effect of Deficiency** | Anergy, hyporesponsiveness                               | Reduced activation           | Reduced activation                   | Exhaustion, hyporesponsiveness | Impaired signaling                  |
| **Rescue by IL-2**       | Yes (IL-2 activates PI3K/Akt)                               | No                          | No                                  | No                             | No                                  |
| **Vignette Clue**        | Decreased CD28/B7 expression, rescue by IL-2             | N/A                         | N/A                                 | N/A                            | N/A                                 |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Full T cell activation requires both TCR engagement (Signal 1) and costimulation (Signal 2, e.g., CD28/B7). Costimulation activates the PI3K/Akt pathway, crucial for proliferation and survival. Lack of costimulation leads to anergy. IL-2 bypasses the need for CD28 by directly activating the PI3K/Akt pathway. Differential: Anergy vs. T cell exhaustion (PD-1). Anergy is caused by lack of costimulation (Signal 2) despite Signal 1, while exhaustion involves chronic antigen exposure and inhibitory receptor upregulation (like PD-1).

<a id='question-052'></a>

### Question 052: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 02 (increase,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: This question tests the understanding of the molecular mechanisms underlying T cell receptor (TCR) signaling, specifically the role of protein tyrosine kinases (PTKs) like Lck and Fyn in initiating the signaling cascade upon antigen recognition, leading to T cell activation. (考察T细胞受体(TCR)信号传导的分子机制，特别是Lck和Fyn等蛋白酪氨酸激酶(PTKs)在抗原识别后启动信号级联反应，从而导致T细胞活化的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, severe headache, and myalgia. He reports being bitten by a tick while hiking in a wooded area of upstate New York two weeks prior. Physical examination reveals a high fever (103°F), diffuse maculopapular rash starting on the trunk and spreading peripherally, and mild splenomegaly. Laboratory tests show leukopenia, thrombocytopenia, and elevated liver enzymes. A skin biopsy from the rash reveals endothelial cell swelling and perivascular lymphocytic infiltrates. Serological testing is positive for antibodies against *Rickettsia rickettsii*. The patient is treated with doxycycline and recovers fully.

Further research into the pathogenesis of *Rickettsia rickettsii* infection reveals that the bacterium infects endothelial cells, causing vasculitis. The host immune response, particularly the activation of CD4+ T helper cells, is crucial for controlling the infection. Studies show that *R. rickettsii* infection leads to a significant increase in the phosphorylation of specific tyrosine residues on intracellular signaling molecules within activated T cells. Which of the following intracellular signaling events is MOST directly responsible for initiating the T cell activation cascade in response to *R. rickettsii* antigens presented by antigen-presenting cells (APCs)?

(A) Activation of protein kinase C (PKC) by diacylglycerol (DAG) and calcium influx.
(B) Phosphorylation of the ZAP-70 protein by the spleen tyrosine kinase (Syk).
(C) Phosphorylation of the immunoreceptor tyrosine-based activation motif (ITAM) on the CD3 complex by Lck.
(D) Binding of CD28 on the T cell to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on the APC, leading to increased MHC class II expression.
(E) Activation of the complement cascade leading to deposition of C3b on the pathogen, enhancing phagocytosis by macrophages.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient presents with symptoms consistent with Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever (RMSF), caused by *Rickettsia rickettsii*, including fever, headache, rash, and tick exposure history.
- The diagnosis is confirmed by serology.
- The question focuses on the T cell activation cascade initiated in response to *R. rickettsii* antigens presented by APCs.
- The key phrase is "initiating the T cell activation cascade". This points towards the very first steps in TCR signaling.
- The vignette mentions increased phosphorylation of tyrosine residues within activated T cells, hinting at the role of tyrosine kinases.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- T cell activation begins with the interaction of the TCR complex on the T cell surface with the peptide-MHC complex on the APC surface.
- The TCR complex consists of the TCR αβ heterodimer, the CD3 complex (γ, δ, ε, ζ chains), and the ζ chain.
- The CD3 complex contains immunoreceptor tyrosine-based activation motifs (ITAMs) in its cytoplasmic tails (specifically on the ζ chain).
- Upon TCR engagement with the peptide-MHC complex, the intracellular tyrosine kinase Lck (a Src family kinase) is recruited to the TCR complex.
- Lck phosphorylates tyrosine residues on the ITAMs of the CD3 ζ chain.
- This phosphorylation creates docking sites for other signaling molecules, including ZAP-70 (another tyrosine kinase).
- ZAP-70 is then activated (often by phosphorylation by Lck or other kinases) and phosphorylates downstream targets, initiating the signaling cascade that leads to T cell activation, proliferation, and cytokine production.
- Therefore, the phosphorylation of ITAMs on the CD3 complex by Lck is the crucial initial step in TCR signaling.

- Option (A) describes the activation of PKC, which is part of the downstream signaling pathway initiated by the TCR, but it is not the *initiating* event. PKC is activated by DAG and calcium, which are produced downstream of the initial TCR signaling events involving ITAM phosphorylation and ZAP-70 activation.
- Option (B) describes the phosphorylation of ZAP-70 by Syk. Syk is primarily involved in B cell receptor (BCR) signaling and Fc receptor signaling, not the initial TCR signaling. ZAP-70 is phosphorylated downstream of ITAM phosphorylation by Lck.
- Option (D) describes the CD28-B7 interaction, which provides a crucial co-stimulatory signal for T cell activation. While essential for full T cell activation, it is not the *initiating* event triggered by antigen recognition via the TCR. The TCR-MHC interaction is the primary signal.
- Option (E) describes complement activation and opsonization, which is part of the innate immune response and can enhance phagocytosis, but it is not directly involved in the *initiation* of the T cell activation cascade via the TCR.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Protein kinase C (PKC) activation is a downstream event in T cell signaling, occurring after initial TCR engagement and ITAM phosphorylation. It is involved in the activation of transcription factors like NF-κB and AP-1, but it is not the initiating step. The question asks for the *initiating* event.
- (B): Spleen tyrosine kinase (Syk) is primarily involved in B cell receptor signaling and Fc receptor signaling. ZAP-70, not Syk, is the key tyrosine kinase activated downstream of ITAM phosphorylation in T cells.
- (D): The CD28-B7 interaction provides a crucial co-stimulatory signal (Signal 2) for T cell activation, preventing anergy. However, the primary signal (Signal 1) that *initiates* the cascade is the TCR engagement with the peptide-MHC complex, leading to ITAM phosphorylation.
- (E): Complement activation is part of the innate immune system and enhances phagocytosis and inflammation. It does not directly initiate the T cell activation cascade via the TCR.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature | Correct Answer (C) | Option (A) - PKC Activation | Option (B) - Syk Phosphorylation | Option (D) - CD28/B7 | Option (E) - Complement |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Process** | Initiating TCR signaling | Downstream TCR signaling | B cell/FcR signaling | Co-stimulation for T cells | Innate immunity |
| **Key Molecule** | Lck, ITAMs, CD3ζ | PKC, DAG, Ca2+ | Syk, ZAP-70 | CD28, B7 (CD80/86) | C3b, C5a |
| **Timing** | First step after TCR engagement | Subsequent to ITAM phosphorylation | Not involved in initial TCR signal | Required for full T cell activation, not initiation | Independent of TCR signaling |
| **Relevance to Question** | Directly initiates T cell activation cascade | Downstream consequence | Incorrect kinase/pathway | Co-stimulatory, not initiating | Unrelated to T cell activation initiation |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The initial signal for T cell activation is the engagement of the TCR complex with the peptide-MHC complex, leading to the phosphorylation of ITAMs on the CD3ζ chain by the tyrosine kinase Lck. This phosphorylation recruits and activates downstream kinases like ZAP-70, initiating the signaling cascade. Remember that Lck is the key kinase responsible for the *initial* phosphorylation event on the ITAMs. Differentiate this initiating step from downstream events like PKC activation or co-stimulatory signals like CD28-B7 interaction. Also, distinguish T cell signaling kinases (Lck, ZAP-70) from B cell signaling kinases (Syk).

<a id='question-053'></a>

### Question 053: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 03 (enhancing)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of costimulatory signals (e.g., CD28-B7 interaction) in T cell activation, specifically how these signals enhance the primary immune response and prevent anergy, and how deficiencies or manipulations of these pathways affect T cell function. (理解共刺激信号（例如CD28-B7相互作用）在T细胞激活中的作用，特别是这些信号如何增强初级免疫应答并防止无能，以及这些途径的缺陷或操纵如何影响T细胞功能。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, myalgia, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. He recently returned from a 2-week trip to rural Thailand, where he was bitten multiple times by mosquitoes. He denies any known allergies or chronic medical conditions. Physical examination reveals a temperature of 38.5°C, tachycardia, and the characteristic rash. Laboratory tests show leukopenia and thrombocytopenia. A skin biopsy from the rash shows endothelial cell swelling and fibrin thrombi in small vessels, consistent with vasculitis. The patient is treated empirically with supportive care. His symptoms worsen over the next 48 hours, leading to multi-organ failure and death. Postmortem analysis of his spleen reveals marked depletion of CD4+ T cells and evidence of widespread viral replication within endothelial cells. Further investigation reveals that the patient was infected with a novel flavivirus transmitted by Aedes mosquitoes. This virus encodes a protein, NS5, which is known to interfere with T cell activation. Specifically, NS5 protein is found to bind to and internalize B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) from the surface of antigen-presenting cells (APCs), preventing their interaction with CD28 on T cells.

Which of the following mechanisms BEST explains the observed T cell depletion and severe clinical outcome in this patient?

(A) The virus directly infects and lyses CD4+ T cells, leading to immunodeficiency.
(B) The virus induces apoptosis of APCs, preventing antigen presentation to T cells.
(C) The virus inhibits the production of IL-2 by T cells, impairing T cell proliferation.
(D) The virus prevents the interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 molecules on APCs, blocking the essential costimulatory signal required for full T cell activation and survival.
(E) The virus blocks the interaction between the T cell receptor (TCR) and MHC-peptide complex on APCs, preventing signal transduction.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** Young adult male with fever, rash, myalgia, leukopenia, thrombocytopenia after travel to Thailand and mosquito bites. This suggests a mosquito-borne viral illness, potentially dengue, chikungunya, or a flavivirus.
- **Clinical Course:** Rapid progression to multi-organ failure and death despite supportive care indicates a severe infection.
- **Pathology:** Skin biopsy shows vasculitis (endothelial swelling, fibrin thrombi). Postmortem spleen shows CD4+ T cell depletion and viral replication in endothelial cells. This points towards a virus causing endothelial damage and immune dysregulation.
- **Viral Mechanism:** The virus encodes NS5 protein, which internalizes B7 (CD80/CD86) molecules from APCs.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining T cell depletion and severe outcome due to the virus's interference with T cell activation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Activation:** Naive T cells require two signals for activation:
    1.  **Signal 1:** TCR binding to the MHC-peptide complex on APCs. This provides antigen specificity.
    2.  **Signal 2 (Costimulation):** CD28 on the T cell binding to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on the APC. This signal is crucial for full T cell activation, proliferation, cytokine production (like IL-2), and survival. Without costimulation, T cells may become anergic (unresponsive) or undergo apoptosis.
- **Role of B7 (CD80/CD86):** These molecules are upregulated on APCs (dendritic cells, macrophages, B cells) upon activation, typically by PAMPs (via TLRs) or inflammatory signals. They provide the essential costimulatory signal via CD28.
- **Role of CD28:** This is a constitutively expressed receptor on naive and memory T cells. Its engagement by B7 is mandatory for robust T cell activation.
- **Viral Evasion:** Many viruses have evolved mechanisms to evade the host immune system. Some viruses target T cell activation pathways.
- **NS5 Protein Mechanism:** The described NS5 protein interferes with T cell activation by targeting B7 molecules. By internalizing B7 from APCs, NS5 prevents the CD28-B7 interaction.
- **Consequences of Blocking CD28-B7:** Without the costimulatory signal, T cells receiving Signal 1 (TCR engagement) may become anergic or undergo apoptosis. This leads to impaired T cell responses, including reduced proliferation, cytokine production (like IL-2, crucial for T cell clonal expansion), and cytotoxic function. The resulting immunodeficiency contributes to uncontrolled viral replication and severe disease. The depletion of CD4+ T cells observed postmortem is consistent with this mechanism.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** While some viruses (like HIV) directly infect and kill CD4+ T cells, the vignette specifically describes a mechanism involving the NS5 protein interfering with T cell *activation* by targeting B7 molecules on APCs, not direct T cell lysis. The T cell depletion is likely secondary to lack of costimulation and subsequent apoptosis or failure to proliferate, rather than direct viral cytolysis.
- (B): **Incorrect.** The vignette states that the virus replicates within endothelial cells, not primarily APCs. While APC function is impaired due to B7 downregulation, the primary target of NS5 is B7 on APCs, not the APCs themselves leading to their apoptosis. Impaired APC function is a consequence, not the primary mechanism described.
- (C): **Incorrect.** IL-2 production is indeed dependent on T cell activation, particularly the costimulatory signal. Blocking CD28-B7 interaction would impair IL-2 production. However, the *primary* defect described is the *blockage of the costimulatory signal itself*. Option (D) describes the root cause of the impaired IL-2 production and other T cell functions. Option (C) describes a downstream consequence, making (D) the more fundamental and accurate explanation.
- (E): **Incorrect.** Signal 1 involves the TCR binding to the MHC-peptide complex. The vignette explicitly states that NS5 targets B7 molecules, which are involved in Signal 2 (costimulation), not Signal 1. Blocking Signal 1 would prevent antigen recognition, but the described mechanism targets costimulation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Correct Mechanism (D)                                  | Distractor (A) - Direct T cell lysis (e.g., HIV) | Distractor (B) - APC apoptosis (e.g., some bacterial toxins) | Distractor (C) - Impaired IL-2 (Downstream effect) | Distractor (E) - Blocked TCR-MHC (e.g., some viral MHC analogs) |
| :------------------ | :----------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------------ | :------------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Primary Target**  | B7 (CD80/CD86) on APCs                                | CD4+ T cells                                      | APCs                                                     | T cells (IL-2 production)                             | TCR-MHC interaction                                            |
| **Affected Signal** | Signal 2 (Costimulation)                               | T cell survival/proliferation                      | Antigen presentation                                     | T cell proliferation/cytokine production             | Signal 1 (Antigen recognition)                                  |
| **Mechanism**       | Prevents CD28-B7 interaction                           | Direct viral killing/infection                      | APC death/dysfunction                                    | Impaired T cell function downstream of activation     | Prevents initial T cell recognition                         |
| **Outcome**         | Anergy, apoptosis, impaired T cell response, depletion | Immunodeficiency, opportunistic infections         | Impaired antigen presentation, weak T cell response      | Weak T cell response                                 | No T cell activation                                           |
| **Example Virus**   | Flavivirus (NS5 protein)                               | HIV                                               | N/A (less common mechanism)                             | N/A (consequence, not primary mechanism)              | CMV (immune evasion)                                           |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The critical role of costimulatory signals (CD28-B7) in T cell activation is a high-yield concept. Lack of costimulation leads to T cell anergy or apoptosis, impairing adaptive immunity. Viral evasion strategies often target these pathways. Differentiate this mechanism from direct T cell lysis (HIV), impaired antigen presentation (APCs), downstream effects like IL-2 deficiency, or blocking the initial TCR-MHC interaction.

<a id='question-054'></a>

### Question 054: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 04 (activation)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the signaling pathways downstream of T cell receptor (TCR) and B cell receptor (BCR) activation, specifically the role of protein kinases like Lck and Syk in initiating intracellular signaling cascades leading to lymphocyte activation, proliferation, and differentiation. (理解T细胞受体 (TCR) 和 B细胞受体 (BCR) 激活后下游的信号通路，特别是 Lck 和 Syk 等蛋白激酶在启动细胞内信号级联反应中，从而导致淋巴细胞活化、增殖和分化中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. He reports recent travel to a rural area in Southeast Asia where he was bitten multiple times by ticks. Physical examination reveals a high fever (39.5°C), generalized lymphadenopathy, and hepatosplenomegaly. Laboratory tests show leukopenia, thrombocytopenia, and elevated liver enzymes. A skin biopsy from the rash reveals endothelial cell swelling and fibrin thrombi in small vessels, consistent with vasculitis. Immunofluorescence staining of the biopsy shows deposition of IgM and C3 along the vessel walls. Further investigation reveals the patient is homozygous for a mutation in the *SH2* domain of the *LCK* gene, which prevents Lck from binding to the immunoreceptor tyrosine-based activation motif (ITAM) on the CD3 complex. Which of the following intracellular signaling events is most likely impaired in this patient, leading to his clinical presentation?

(A) Activation of phospholipase C gamma (PLCγ) by phosphorylation of its SH2 domain.
(B) Recruitment and activation of Bruton's tyrosine kinase (BTK) via phosphorylation of its SH2 domain.
(C) Phosphorylation of the immunoreceptor tyrosine-based inhibitory motif (ITIM) on CD22.
(D) Activation of spleen tyrosine kinase (Syk) by phosphorylation of its SH2 domain.
(E) Recruitment of Grb2 via its SH2 domain to phosphorylated tyrosine residues on the CD3 complex.

#### Correct Answer: (E)

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old male with fever, chills, rash, lymphadenopathy, hepatosplenomegaly after tick bites in Southeast Asia. This suggests a tick-borne illness, potentially rickettsial or viral.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Leukopenia, thrombocytopenia, elevated liver enzymes are common in severe systemic infections like Rocky Mountain Spotted Fever (RMSF) or Dengue fever.
- **Skin Biopsy:** Vasculitis (endothelial swelling, fibrin thrombi) with IgM and C3 deposition is characteristic of immune complex-mediated vasculitis, often seen in RMSF.
- **Genetic Defect:** Homozygous mutation in the *SH2* domain of *LCK* prevents binding to ITAMs on CD3. Lck is a tyrosine kinase crucial for T cell activation.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the impaired intracellular signaling event due to the Lck mutation.

The clinical picture strongly suggests RMSF, caused by *Rickettsia rickettsii*. RMSF involves endothelial cell infection leading to vasculitis and immune complex deposition. However, the core of the question focuses on the immunological defect caused by the Lck mutation. Lck is a non-receptor tyrosine kinase belonging to the Src family. It is essential for T cell activation. Upon TCR engagement, Lck phosphorylates tyrosine residues on the ITAMs of the CD3 complex (specifically CD3ζ, CD3ε, CD3δ chains). This phosphorylation creates docking sites for SH2 domains of downstream signaling molecules. The question asks which specific event is impaired due to the Lck mutation preventing ITAM phosphorylation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **TCR Signaling:** The T cell receptor (TCR) complex, associated with the CD3 complex (γ, δ, ε, ζ chains), initiates signaling upon antigen recognition presented by MHC molecules.
- **Lck's Role:** Lck is constitutively associated with the cytoplasmic tails of the TCR ζ chain via its SH2 domain. Upon TCR engagement, Lck is activated (autophosphorylated on tyrosine residues) and phosphorylates the ITAMs (YxxL/V motifs) on the CD3ζ, CD3ε, and CD3δ chains.
- **Downstream Signaling:** Phosphorylated ITAMs serve as docking sites for SH2 domains of other signaling molecules. Key molecules recruited include:
    - **ZAP-70:** A tyrosine kinase that phosphorylates downstream targets like LAT (Linker for Activation of T cells).
    - **Grb2:** An adaptor protein containing SH2 and SH3 domains. Its SH2 domain binds to phosphorylated ITAMs, and its SH3 domain binds to SOS (Son of Sevenless), a guanine nucleotide exchange factor (GEF) for Ras. This leads to Ras activation, initiating the MAPK pathway.
    - **PLCγ:** An enzyme that hydrolyzes PIP2 into IP3 and DAG, leading to calcium release and protein kinase C (PKC) activation.
- **The Lck Mutation:** The mutation in the SH2 domain of Lck prevents it from binding to the ITAMs on the CD3 complex. Consequently, Lck cannot phosphorylate these ITAMs upon TCR stimulation. This blocks the recruitment of downstream signaling molecules that rely on binding to phosphorylated ITAMs via their SH2 domains.
- **Analyzing the Options:**
    - (A) PLCγ activation requires phosphorylation of its SH2 domain, which occurs downstream of ITAM phosphorylation and recruitment of other kinases like ZAP-70 or Syk (in B cells). Since ITAM phosphorylation is blocked, PLCγ activation is impaired, but the *direct* consequence of Lck's inability to phosphorylate ITAMs is the failure to recruit molecules that bind to *phosphorylated* ITAMs.
    - (B) BTK is crucial for B cell receptor (BCR) signaling, not TCR signaling. It is activated via phosphorylation of its SH2 domain by Syk. This is irrelevant to the Lck defect in T cells.
    - (C) Phosphorylation of ITIMs (containing YxxΦ motifs) on inhibitory receptors like CD22 leads to recruitment of phosphatases like SHP-1, which *inhibit* signaling. This is not the primary event initiated by TCR activation and is not directly dependent on Lck's ITAM phosphorylation activity.
    - (D) Syk is the primary tyrosine kinase involved in BCR signaling, analogous to Lck in TCR signaling. Syk phosphorylates ITAMs on Igα/Igβ chains of the BCR. This is irrelevant to the Lck defect in T cells.
    - (E) Grb2 contains an SH2 domain that binds to phosphorylated tyrosine residues on the CD3 complex (specifically the ITAMs phosphorylated by Lck). Since Lck cannot phosphorylate these ITAMs due to the SH2 domain mutation, Grb2 cannot be recruited. This directly reflects the consequence of the Lck defect.

Therefore, the most direct consequence of the Lck mutation preventing ITAM phosphorylation is the failure to recruit Grb2 via its SH2 domain. This blocks the Ras/MAPK pathway, a critical branch of T cell activation signaling. The patient's clinical presentation (likely RMSF) is a separate issue, but the question specifically asks about the immunological defect caused by the Lck mutation.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Activation of PLCγ is indeed impaired downstream of Lck, but it relies on the initial phosphorylation of ITAMs by Lck and subsequent recruitment/activation of other kinases. The *most direct* consequence of Lck's inability to phosphorylate ITAMs is the failure to recruit molecules that bind to *phosphorylated* ITAMs. Grb2 recruitment is a key early step blocked by the Lck defect.
- (B): BTK is involved in B cell signaling, specifically the BCR pathway. Lck is specific to T cell signaling (TCR pathway). This option confuses T cell and B cell signaling pathways.
- (C): ITIM phosphorylation and SHP-1 recruitment are involved in *inhibitory* signaling pathways, often triggered by engagement of inhibitory receptors. Lck's primary role is in *activating* signaling via ITAM phosphorylation. This option confuses activating and inhibitory signals.
- (D): Syk is the tyrosine kinase analogous to Lck but functions in B cell signaling (BCR pathway), not T cell signaling (TCR pathway). This option confuses T cell and B cell signaling pathways.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Lck Signaling Defect (TCR)                                  | Syk Signaling Defect (BCR)                                  | BTK Signaling Defect (BCR)                                  | Grb2 Defect (General)                                       |
|----------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------|
| **Cell Type**        | T cells                                                     | B cells                                                     | B cells                                                     | All cells using Ras pathway                                  |
| **Receptor**         | TCR/CD3                                                     | BCR (Igα/Igβ)                                               | BCR (Igα/Igβ)                                               | Various receptors (EGFR, PDGFR, TCR, BCR)                    |
| **Key Kinase**       | Lck                                                         | Syk                                                         | BTK                                                         | N/A (Adaptor protein)                                        |
| **ITAM Phosphorylation** | Yes (CD3ζ, ε, δ)                                           | Yes (Igα/Igβ)                                               | No (BTK phosphorylates Igα/Igβ downstream of Syk)            | N/A                                                         |
| **Downstream Target** | ZAP-70, LAT, Grb2, PLCγ                                      | ZAP-70, LAT, Grb2, PLCγ                                      | Syk, ZAP-70, LAT, Grb2, PLCγ                                  | SOS (Ras activation), PI3K                                   |
| **Defect Consequence**| Impaired T cell activation, proliferation, cytokine production | Impaired B cell activation, proliferation, antibody production | Impaired B cell development, activation, antibody production | Impaired Ras activation, cell growth, differentiation        |
| **Associated Disease**| Immunodeficiency (e.g., SCID)                               | Immunodeficiency (e.g., XLA)                               | Immunodeficiency (e.g., XLA)                               | Cancer (e.g., some leukemias)                               |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The Lck tyrosine kinase is essential for T cell activation. It phosphorylates ITAMs on the CD3 complex, recruiting downstream signaling molecules like ZAP-70 and Grb2. Mutations affecting Lck function (e.g., in the SH2 domain preventing ITAM binding or kinase activity) lead to impaired T cell signaling and immunodeficiency. Remember the key players in TCR vs. BCR signaling: Lck/ZAP-70 vs. Syk/BTK. Differentiate between ITAMs (activating) and ITIMs (inhibitory) signaling.

<a id='question-055'></a>

### Question 055: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 05 (mature,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of CD40 ligand (CD40L) on activated T cells in providing co-stimulation for B cell activation, isotype switching, and affinity maturation, particularly in the context of T-dependent antibody responses. (理解激活的 T 细胞上 CD40 配体 (CD40L) 在 B 细胞激活、同种异型转换和亲和力成熟中的共刺激作用，特别是在 T 依赖性抗体应答的背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 6-year-old boy presents to his pediatrician with recurrent sinopulmonary infections, including pneumonia and otitis media, starting around 1 year of age. He has a history of severe varicella infection at age 3, complicated by pneumonia. His mother reports that he does not respond well to polysaccharide vaccines (e.g., pneumococcal vaccine). Physical examination reveals normal lymph nodes and spleen size. Laboratory studies show normal total immunoglobulin levels (IgG, IgA, IgM, IgE), but markedly reduced antibody responses to new antigens (e.g., tetanus toxoid vaccine). Flow cytometry analysis of his peripheral blood lymphocytes reveals normal numbers of B cells and T cells, with normal expression of surface markers like CD3, CD4, CD8, CD19, and CD20. However, his T cells show normal expression of CD40L (CD154) but his B cells lack CD40 expression. Genetic testing confirms a homozygous mutation in the *CD40* gene.

Which of the following cellular interactions is most likely impaired in this patient, leading to his clinical presentation?

(A) Interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 (CD80/CD86) on antigen-presenting cells (APCs).
(B) Interaction between Fas ligand (FasL) on activated T cells and Fas receptor on B cells.
(C) Interaction between CD40 ligand (CD40L) on activated T helper cells and CD40 on B cells.
(D) Interaction between programmed death-1 (PD-1) on T cells and PD-L1 on B cells.
(E) Interaction between CD19 on B cells and CD21 on follicular dendritic cells (FDCs).

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Recurrent sinopulmonary infections:** Suggests impaired humoral immunity, specifically antibody production against encapsulated bacteria.
- **Severe varicella:** Indicates a defect in cell-mediated immunity or antibody responses against viruses.
- **Poor response to polysaccharide vaccines:** Polysaccharide antigens are T-independent antigens, primarily eliciting IgM responses. The patient's normal total Ig levels but poor response to *new* antigens (like tetanus toxoid, a protein antigen) points towards a defect in T-dependent antibody responses (isotype switching, affinity maturation, memory B cell formation).
- **Normal total Ig levels:** Suggests that the patient can still produce some antibodies, possibly through T-independent mechanisms or residual T-dependent function, but the defect is primarily in the quality and specificity of the antibody response.
- **Normal B and T cell numbers and surface markers (except CD40 on B cells):** Rules out general lymphopenia or defects in early B/T cell development (e.g., SCID, XLA). The specific lack of CD40 on B cells is a crucial clue.
- **Homozygous *CD40* gene mutation:** Confirms the diagnosis of Hyper-IgM syndrome type 1 (HIGM1), caused by defective CD40 on B cells.
- **Question asks for the impaired cellular interaction:** The core defect is the inability of B cells to receive the necessary co-stimulatory signal from T helper cells due to the lack of the CD40 receptor.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T-dependent B cell activation:** Requires two signals. Signal 1 is BCR engagement with antigen (often with T cell help). Signal 2 is co-stimulation, primarily provided by CD40 on B cells interacting with CD40L (CD154) on activated T helper cells.
- **CD40-CD40L interaction:** This interaction is crucial for B cell proliferation, isotype switching (from IgM to IgG, IgA, IgE), affinity maturation (somatic hypermutation followed by selection), and the formation of memory B cells.
- **HIGM1 (CD40 deficiency):** Patients lack functional CD40 on B cells. This prevents them from receiving the essential co-stimulatory signal (Signal 2) from T helper cells. Consequently, they cannot undergo isotype switching or affinity maturation effectively. They primarily produce IgM antibodies, often at normal or elevated levels, but lack IgG, IgA, and IgE. This leads to susceptibility to infections, particularly by encapsulated bacteria (due to lack of IgG opsonization and complement activation) and opportunistic pathogens like *Pneumocystis jirovecii* (due to impaired T cell help). The poor response to polysaccharide vaccines is also characteristic, although T-independent responses can still occur to some extent, explaining the normal total IgM. The inability to mount effective responses to protein antigens (like tetanus toxoid) highlights the defect in T-dependent immunity.
- **CD28-B7 interaction:** This is a crucial co-stimulatory interaction between T cells and APCs (like B cells or dendritic cells) during T cell activation (Signal 2 for T cells). It is not the primary defect in HIGM1.
- **FasL-Fas interaction:** This interaction mediates apoptosis. While important in immune regulation, it's not the primary pathway affected in HIGM1.
- **PD-1-PD-L1 interaction:** This is an inhibitory pathway involved in T cell exhaustion and immune tolerance. It's not the primary defect in HIGM1.
- **CD19-CD21 interaction:** CD19 is a B cell co-receptor, and CD21 (CR2) is the complement receptor 2 found on B cells and FDCs. This interaction is involved in B cell activation, particularly in response to complement-coated antigens, and in B cell localization within follicles. It is not the primary defect in HIGM1.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Interaction between CD28 on T cells and B7 (CD80/CD86) on antigen-presenting cells (APCs). This interaction is essential for T cell activation (Signal 2). A defect here would cause T cell anergy or dysfunction, leading to impaired T cell help for B cells, but it doesn't explain the specific lack of CD40 on B cells or the HIGM phenotype. This is incorrect.
- (B): Interaction between Fas ligand (FasL) on activated T cells and Fas receptor on B cells. This interaction is involved in regulating immune responses, often leading to apoptosis of activated cells. Defects in this pathway are associated with autoimmune diseases (e.g., Fas deficiency) or lymphoproliferation, not HIGM syndrome. This is incorrect.
- (D): Interaction between programmed death-1 (PD-1) on T cells and PD-L1 on B cells. This is an inhibitory checkpoint pathway. Blocking this pathway can enhance T cell responses, particularly in cancer immunotherapy. It is not the primary defect in HIGM1. This is incorrect.
- (E): Interaction between CD19 on B cells and CD21 on follicular dendritic cells (FDCs). CD19 is part of the B cell receptor complex and enhances BCR signaling. CD21 binds complement components and helps retain B cells in follicles. Defects in CD19 or CD21 can cause B cell deficiencies, but not the specific HIGM phenotype characterized by a lack of isotype switching due to defective CD40-CD40L interaction. This is incorrect.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | HIGM1 (CD40 Deficiency) | X-linked Agammaglobulinemia (XLA) | Common Variable Immunodeficiency (CVID) | DiGeorge Syndrome |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------- | :-------------------------------- | :-------------------------------------- | :---------------- |
| **Primary Defect**      | CD40 on B cells         | BTK (B cell development)          | Variable (often BAFF-R, ICOS, CD19)     | T cell development (Thymus) |
| **B Cell Numbers**      | Normal                  | Absent/Very Low                   | Normal/Low                              | Normal            |
| **T Cell Numbers**      | Normal                  | Normal                            | Normal                                  | Low/Absent        |
| **Ig Levels**           | Normal/High IgM, Low IgG/A/E | Very Low all Ig classes           | Low IgG, Variable IgA/IgM/IgE           | Variable         |
| **Isotype Switching**   | Defective               | Defective (due to lack of B cells) | Defective                                | Normal (usually)  |
| **Response to Vaccines** | Poor (T-dependent)      | Poor (no B cells)                 | Poor                                    | Poor (T cell help) |
| **Key Infections**      | Encapsulated bacteria, *Pneumocystis* | Encapsulated bacteria            | Encapsulated bacteria, viruses          | Fungal, Viral     |
| **CD40L Expression**    | Normal                  | Normal                            | Normal                                  | Normal            |
| **CD40 Expression**     | Defective               | Normal                            | Normal                                  | Normal            |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The inability of B cells to express CD40 leads to Hyper-IgM syndrome type 1 (HIGM1), characterized by defective T-dependent antibody responses (isotype switching, affinity maturation, memory formation) despite normal B cell numbers and total IgM levels. This highlights the critical role of CD40-CD40L interaction in providing essential co-stimulation for B cell activation. Differentiate HIGM1 from other B cell deficiencies like XLA (lack of B cells) and CVID (variable defects) based on specific immunoglobulin patterns and cellular markers.

<a id='question-056'></a>

### Question 056: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 06 (cells.)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific cell surface receptors and intracellular signaling pathways in T cell activation, specifically focusing on the interaction between the T cell receptor (TCR) complex and the peptide-MHC complex, and the subsequent downstream signaling cascade involving CD3 and ZAP-70. (理解T细胞激活中T细胞受体复合物与肽-MHC复合物的相互作用，以及随后的CD3和ZAP-70参与的下游信号传导级联反应。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, myalgia, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. He reports traveling to a rural area of Southeast Asia two weeks prior for a volunteer mission. He denies any known allergies or recent vaccinations. Physical examination reveals a temperature of 38.5°C (101.3°F), blood pressure of 110/70 mmHg, and heart rate of 90 bpm. The rash is non-pruritic and covers his trunk and extremities. Laboratory tests show leukopenia (WBC count 2,500/µL) with lymphopenia, elevated liver enzymes (AST 150 U/L, ALT 180 U/L), and a positive serology for Dengue virus IgM antibodies. A skin biopsy from the rash shows perivascular lymphocytic infiltrates. The patient's symptoms resolve spontaneously within a week.

Which of the following intracellular signaling molecules is MOST directly involved in the initial signal transduction cascade following engagement of the T cell receptor (TCR) complex by a peptide-MHC complex?

(A) Phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase (PI3K)
(B) Mitogen-activated protein kinase kinase kinase (MAPKKK)
(C) Zymogen-activated protein kinase 70 (ZAP-70)
(D) Interleukin-2 receptor beta chain (IL-2Rβ)
(E) Signal transducer and activator of transcription 3 (STAT3)

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient presents with symptoms consistent with Dengue fever (fever, rash, myalgia, leukopenia, lymphopenia, elevated liver enzymes) after travel to an endemic area.
- The question asks about the initial intracellular signaling cascade following TCR engagement.
- While the clinical vignette describes Dengue fever, which involves an immune response, the core question is about fundamental T cell activation signaling, independent of the specific pathogen. The clinical context serves as background.
- The key phrase is "initial signal transduction cascade following engagement of the T cell receptor (TCR) complex by a peptide-MHC complex". This points directly to the first signal in T cell activation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- T cell activation requires two signals. Signal 1 is initiated by the interaction between the TCR complex on the T cell surface and the peptide-MHC complex on the antigen-presenting cell (APC).
- The TCR complex consists of the TCR αβ heterodimer, the CD3 complex (γ, δ, ε, ζ chains), and the CD4 or CD8 co-receptor.
- Upon binding to the peptide-MHC complex, the TCR complex undergoes a conformational change, leading to the phosphorylation of tyrosine residues on the intracellular domains of the CD3 chains (specifically the ζ chain).
- This phosphorylation recruits and activates intracellular tyrosine kinases, primarily ZAP-70 (also known as TCK, T-cell kinase).
- ZAP-70 then phosphorylates other downstream signaling molecules, including LAT (Linker for Activation of T cells), leading to the activation of various signaling pathways (e.g., PLCγ, MAPK pathways) that ultimately result in T cell activation, proliferation, and cytokine production.
- Signal 2 is provided by co-stimulatory molecules (e.g., CD28 on T cells binding to B7 molecules on APCs) and cytokines (e.g., IL-2).
- The question specifically asks about the *initial* signal transduction cascade, which is mediated by ZAP-70 following TCR engagement.

##### 3. Analysis of Options
- (A) PI3K: Phosphatidylinositol 3-kinase is involved in downstream signaling pathways activated by both TCR and co-stimulatory signals (like CD28), particularly important for cell survival and proliferation via the Akt pathway. It is not the *most direct* kinase activated immediately following TCR engagement.
- (B) MAPKKK: Mitogen-activated protein kinase kinase kinases (e.g., Ras, Raf) are part of the MAPK pathway, which is activated downstream of TCR signaling, but ZAP-70 is the primary kinase directly activated by the TCR complex.
- (C) ZAP-70: Zymogen-activated protein kinase 70 (also known as TCK) is the primary tyrosine kinase directly recruited and activated by the phosphorylated CD3 complex upon TCR engagement with the peptide-MHC complex. It is the crucial first step in Signal 1 transduction.
- (D) IL-2Rβ: The IL-2 receptor beta chain (CD122) is part of the high-affinity IL-2 receptor complex. IL-2 signaling is crucial for T cell proliferation and survival (Signal 2 component), but the receptor itself is not directly involved in the *initial* TCR signaling cascade.
- (E) STAT3: Signal transducer and activator of transcription 3 (STAT3) is a transcription factor activated downstream of cytokine receptor signaling, particularly IL-6 and IL-21 signaling, and also plays a role in Treg development. It is not directly involved in the initial TCR signaling cascade.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) PI3K: Incorrect. PI3K is downstream of both TCR and co-stimulation signals and is crucial for cell survival and proliferation, but not the *initial* kinase activated by TCR engagement. Test-takers might confuse it with downstream signaling events.
- (B) MAPKKK: Incorrect. MAPKKKs are part of the MAPK pathway, which is activated downstream of ZAP-70 and LAT phosphorylation. Test-takers might confuse it with the broader MAPK signaling cascade.
- (D) IL-2Rβ: Incorrect. This is a cytokine receptor component involved in T cell proliferation (Signal 2), not the initial TCR signaling pathway (Signal 1). Test-takers might confuse the different signals required for T cell activation.
- (E) STAT3: Incorrect. STAT3 is a transcription factor activated by cytokine signaling (e.g., IL-6), not directly by the TCR complex. Test-takers might confuse it with downstream transcriptional regulation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | ZAP-70 (Correct)                                   | PI3K (Distractor A)                                  | MAPKKK (Distractor B)                                | IL-2Rβ (Distractor D)                               | STAT3 (Distractor E)                                |
|----------------------|----------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------------------|
| **Role**             | Initial TCR signal transduction (Signal 1)          | Downstream signaling (survival, proliferation)        | Downstream signaling (MAPK pathway)                  | Cytokine receptor (Signal 2)                         | Transcription factor (cytokine signaling)            |
| **Activation Trigger**| TCR engagement with peptide-MHC                  | TCR + Co-stimulation                                 | Downstream of ZAP-70/LAT                             | IL-2 binding                                         | Cytokine binding (e.g., IL-6)                        |
| **Location**         | Intracellular, associated with CD3 complex        | Cytoplasmic, associated with plasma membrane          | Cytoplasmic                                           | Transmembrane receptor                                | Nuclear (after activation)                           |
| **Pathway**          | TCR -> CD3 phosphorylation -> ZAP-70 activation -> LAT | TCR/CD28 -> PI3K -> Akt -> Survival/Proliferation | TCR -> ZAP-70/LAT -> Ras/Raf/MEK -> ERK (MAPK)        | IL-2 -> JAK -> STAT5 -> Gene transcription           | Cytokine -> JAK -> STAT3 -> Gene transcription        |
| **Key Function**     | Phosphorylates downstream targets (e.g., LAT)      | Phosphorylates PIP2 to PIP3                          | Activates MAPKs                                       | Binds IL-2, initiates signaling                      | Regulates gene expression in response to cytokines |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The initial signal for T cell activation (Signal 1) is transduced through the TCR complex, leading to the activation of the tyrosine kinase ZAP-70. This is a critical step distinguishing it from downstream events or components of Signal 2 (co-stimulation/cytokines). Remember the sequence: TCR engagement -> CD3 phosphorylation -> ZAP-70 activation -> downstream signaling (LAT, PLCγ, MAPK). Key differentials include downstream kinases (PI3K, MAPKKK), components of Signal 2 (IL-2Rβ, STAT3), and other signaling molecules not directly involved in the initial TCR signal.

<a id='question-057'></a>

### Question 057: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 07 (immune)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of specific signaling molecules (like Syk) in B cell receptor (BCR) signaling and its downstream effects on B cell activation, proliferation, and differentiation, particularly in the context of antibody production. (理解BCR信号通路中Syk激酶的作用及其下游对B细胞活化、增殖和分化的影响，尤其是在抗体生成背景下。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old female presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, myalgia, and a diffuse maculopapular rash. She recently returned from a 2-week trip to Southeast Asia, where she was bitten multiple times by mosquitoes. Laboratory tests reveal thrombocytopenia, hyponatremia, and elevated liver enzymes. A skin biopsy from the rash shows endothelial cell swelling and fibrin thrombi in small vessels. Serological testing is positive for IgM antibodies against Dengue virus, but IgG antibodies are negative. The patient's B cells are isolated and stimulated *in vitro* with anti-IgM antibodies. Flow cytometry analysis of these stimulated B cells shows decreased phosphorylation of a specific tyrosine kinase compared to control B cells.

Which of the following intracellular signaling molecules is most likely deficient in this patient's B cells, leading to impaired B cell activation?

(A) Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase (BTK)
(B) Syk Tyrosine Kinase
(C) ZAP-70 Tyrosine Kinase
(D) PI3K (Phosphatidylinositol 3-Kinase)
(E) Akt (Protein Kinase B)

#### Correct Answer: B

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old female with fever, chills, myalgia, rash after travel to Southeast Asia (Dengue endemic area) and mosquito bites. This points towards an acute viral infection, likely Dengue fever.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Thrombocytopenia, hyponatremia, elevated liver enzymes are characteristic features of severe Dengue fever.
- **Serology:** Positive IgM, negative IgG indicates an acute primary Dengue infection. IgM is the first antibody produced.
- **B Cell Stimulation & Flow Cytometry:** *In vitro* stimulation of B cells with anti-IgM (mimicking antigen binding to BCR) shows decreased phosphorylation of a specific tyrosine kinase. This indicates a defect in the B cell signaling pathway downstream of the BCR.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the specific intracellular signaling molecule deficient in the patient's B cells, causing impaired activation.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **BCR Signaling Pathway:** The B cell receptor (BCR) is a complex of membrane-bound immunoglobulins (IgM/IgD) associated with signaling molecules. Antigen binding cross-links the BCR, initiating a signaling cascade.
- **Initial Steps:** Cross-linking leads to the activation of Src family kinases (like Lyn). Lyn phosphorylates immunoreceptor tyrosine-based activation motifs (ITAMs) on the cytoplasmic tails of the Igα/Igβ subunits of the BCR complex.
- **Syk Recruitment:** Phosphorylated ITAMs recruit and activate Syk (Spleen Tyrosine Kinase). Syk is crucial for downstream signaling.
- **Downstream Events:** Activated Syk phosphorylates multiple substrates, including:
    - **BLNK (B Lymphocyte Linker Protein):** An adaptor protein essential for further signaling.
    - **PI3K:** Activates the PI3K/Akt pathway, promoting cell survival and proliferation.
    - **PLCγ2 (Phospholipase C gamma 2):** Leads to calcium mobilization and activation of NF-κB and AP-1 transcription factors, crucial for B cell activation, proliferation, and differentiation.
- **Clinical Context:** The patient has Dengue fever, an acute viral infection. While Dengue itself doesn't directly cause a primary B cell signaling defect, the question focuses on the *in vitro* B cell response. The finding of decreased phosphorylation of a tyrosine kinase upon anti-IgM stimulation points to a defect in the BCR signaling pathway.
- **Analyzing the Options:**
    - **(A) BTK:** Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase is essential for B cell development, particularly the transition from pro-B to pre-B cells in the bone marrow. Mutations cause X-linked agammaglobulinemia (XLA), characterized by a lack of mature B cells and antibodies. This patient has B cells and produces IgM, ruling out BTK deficiency.
    - **(B) Syk:** Syk is directly recruited to the phosphorylated ITAMs of the BCR complex and is the central kinase in BCR signaling after initial Lyn activation. Its phosphorylation is a key early event. Decreased Syk phosphorylation upon BCR stimulation directly impairs downstream signaling, leading to defective B cell activation, proliferation, and antibody production. This fits the flow cytometry findings.
    - **(C) ZAP-70:** ZAP-70 (Zeta-chain-associated protein kinase 70) is a cytoplasmic tyrosine kinase primarily involved in T cell receptor (TCR) signaling, not BCR signaling. It associates with the ZAP-70/CD3ζ complex in the TCR.
    - **(D) PI3K:** PI3K is a downstream effector of Syk. While important for B cell survival and proliferation, Syk must be active first to activate PI3K. Decreased Syk phosphorylation would lead to decreased PI3K activation, but Syk is the more proximal and direct kinase affected in the initial BCR signaling cascade described. The question asks for the *most likely* deficient molecule based on the *initial* signaling defect detected.
    - **(E) Akt:** Akt (Protein Kinase B) is a downstream kinase in the PI3K pathway. It is involved in cell survival, growth, and metabolism. Similar to PI3K, it is downstream of Syk and would be affected secondarily if Syk were deficient.

- **Conclusion:** The decreased phosphorylation of a tyrosine kinase upon anti-IgM stimulation specifically points to a defect in the early BCR signaling pathway. Syk is the key tyrosine kinase directly activated after ITAM phosphorylation and is essential for propagating the signal. Therefore, decreased Syk phosphorylation is the most likely finding.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- **(A) Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase (BTK):** Incorrect. BTK is crucial for B cell *development* in the bone marrow. Deficiency leads to XLA, characterized by the absence of mature B cells. This patient has B cells and is producing IgM, indicating normal B cell development but potentially impaired activation. The defect is in signaling, not development.
- **(B) Syk Tyrosine Kinase:** Correct. Syk is the central kinase in BCR signaling, directly recruited and activated after ITAM phosphorylation. Decreased Syk phosphorylation directly explains the impaired B cell activation observed *in vitro*.
- **(C) ZAP-70 Tyrosine Kinase:** Incorrect. ZAP-70 is primarily involved in T cell receptor (TCR) signaling, not BCR signaling. It is associated with the CD3 complex in T cells.
- **(D) PI3K (Phosphatidylinositol 3-Kinase):** Incorrect. PI3K is a downstream effector of Syk in the BCR pathway. While its activity would be reduced if Syk is deficient, Syk is the more proximal kinase directly involved in initiating the signaling cascade after BCR engagement. The flow cytometry likely detected the defect at the level of Syk itself.
- **(E) Akt (Protein Kinase B):** Incorrect. Akt is even further downstream than PI3K in the PI3K/Akt pathway. It is involved in cell survival and proliferation. A defect at this level would also be secondary to a defect in Syk or PI3K.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature             | Syk Deficiency                                  | BTK Deficiency (XLA)                         | ZAP-70 Deficiency (SCID)                     | PI3K/Akt Pathway Defects                     |
|----------------------|-------------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|----------------------------------------------|
| **Cell Type Affected** | Mature B cells                                  | Immature B cells (pre-B cell stage)          | T cells                                      | Various cells (B, T, others)                 |
| **Primary Defect**    | BCR signaling after antigen binding             | B cell development                             | TCR signaling                                | Cell survival, growth, metabolism             |
| **Clinical Manifestation** | Impaired B cell activation, antibody response | Absent mature B cells, agammaglobulinemia    | Severe combined immunodeficiency (SCID)     | Variable, often immunodeficiency, cancer risk |
| **BCR/TCR Signaling** | Defective BCR signaling (downstream of ITAMs) | Defective B cell development (upstream of BCR) | Defective TCR signaling                       | Defective downstream signaling                |
| **Key Molecule**      | Syk Tyrosine Kinase                             | Bruton's Tyrosine Kinase                     | ZAP-70 Tyrosine Kinase                        | PI3K, Akt                                    |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The BCR signaling pathway involves sequential activation of tyrosine kinases. Following antigen binding and ITAM phosphorylation, Syk is recruited and activated, initiating downstream signaling cascades crucial for B cell activation. Defects in Syk lead to impaired BCR signaling and antibody production. Differentiate Syk deficiency (impaired activation of mature B cells) from BTK deficiency (impaired B cell development) and ZAP-70 deficiency (impaired T cell signaling). Remember that PI3K and Akt are downstream effectors of Syk.

<a id='question-058'></a>

### Question 058: B and T Lymphocyte Activation: Part 2 Focus 08 (response,)
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Understanding the role of costimulatory signals (specifically CD28-B7 interaction) in T cell activation and the consequences of their absence or blockade, leading to anergy or tolerance. (理解共刺激信号 (特别是 CD28-B7 相互作用) 在 T 细胞活化中的作用，以及其缺失或阻断的后果，导致无能或耐受。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 32-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of fever, chills, headache, and myalgia. He recently returned from a 2-week trip to a rural area in Southeast Asia where he was bitten multiple times by mosquitoes. Physical examination reveals a temperature of 39.5°C, diffuse maculopapular rash, and mild splenomegaly. Laboratory tests show leukopenia and thrombocytopenia. A blood smear reveals atypical lymphocytes. Serological testing is positive for IgM antibodies against Dengue virus. The patient is treated symptomatically with supportive care. However, despite the presence of viral antigens and IgM antibodies indicating an active infection, the patient's T cell response, specifically CD4+ T cell proliferation in response to Dengue virus peptides, is markedly diminished. Flow cytometry analysis of his T cells reveals normal expression of the T cell receptor (TCR) and CD3, but significantly reduced expression of CD28.

Which of the following mechanisms BEST explains the diminished CD4+ T cell proliferation observed in this patient?

(A) Downregulation of MHC class II expression on antigen-presenting cells (APCs) due to viral interference.
(B) Impaired processing and presentation of viral antigens by APCs due to defects in proteasomal function.
(C) Lack of costimulatory signaling due to reduced CD28 expression on T cells, preventing full activation.
(D) Production of immunosuppressive cytokines, such as IL-10 and TGF-β, by regulatory T cells (Tregs) that inhibit T cell proliferation.
(E) Apoptosis of activated T cells induced by Fas-FasL interaction triggered by high levels of viral antigens.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Mechanism & Clinical/Microbiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 32-year-old male with fever, chills, headache, myalgia, rash, splenomegaly after travel to Southeast Asia with mosquito bites. This points towards a mosquito-borne viral illness, likely Dengue fever given the symptoms and IgM positivity.
- **Laboratory Findings:** Leukopenia, thrombocytopenia, atypical lymphocytes are characteristic of Dengue fever. IgM antibodies confirm recent infection.
- **Immunological Findings:** Despite evidence of active Dengue infection (viral antigens, IgM), CD4+ T cell proliferation in response to Dengue peptides is diminished. Flow cytometry shows normal TCR/CD3 but reduced CD28 expression on T cells.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the diminished T cell proliferation.
- **Key Clue:** The core finding is impaired T cell proliferation despite active infection, coupled with reduced CD28 expression on T cells. CD28 is a crucial costimulatory molecule on T cells that binds to B7 molecules (CD80/CD86) on APCs. This interaction provides the second signal required for full T cell activation, proliferation, and cytokine production, alongside the first signal (TCR engagement with MHC-peptide complex). Reduced CD28 expression suggests a defect in this costimulatory pathway.

##### 2. Immunological, Pathological & Virulence Deep-Dive (免疫机制、微生物致病机理与分子生物学深度剖析)
- **T Cell Activation:** Naive T cells require two signals for activation:
    1.  Signal 1: TCR binding to peptide-MHC complex on APCs.
    2.  Signal 2 (Costimulation): CD28 on T cells binding to B7 (CD80/CD86) on APCs.
- **Role of CD28-B7:** This interaction is essential for full T cell activation, leading to proliferation, differentiation, and cytokine production (e.g., IL-2). Without costimulation, T cells may become anergic (unresponsive) or undergo apoptosis.
- **Dengue Virus and T Cells:** Dengue virus infection can affect T cell responses. While the exact mechanism for reduced CD28 expression in this specific scenario isn't fully elucidated in standard textbooks as a direct Dengue effect, the principle of costimulation is fundamental. Some viral infections can interfere with APC function or directly affect T cell costimulatory molecules.
- **Anergy:** If a T cell receives Signal 1 (TCR engagement) but not Signal 2 (costimulation), it can become anergic, meaning it is functionally unresponsive to subsequent antigen stimulation, even if costimulation is provided later. This is a mechanism of peripheral tolerance.
- **Clinical Relevance:** Impaired T cell responses can contribute to the pathogenesis or resolution of viral infections. In this case, the reduced CD28 expression directly impairs the costimulatory signal needed for robust CD4+ T cell proliferation against the Dengue virus.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A) Downregulation of MHC class II expression on APCs: While some viruses can interfere with MHC expression, this would primarily affect the presentation of exogenous antigens (like viral proteins) to CD4+ T cells (Signal 1). The vignette specifically points to a problem with T cell *proliferation* despite antigen presentation (indicated by IgM response), suggesting the primary defect is in the T cell's response *after* Signal 1, which is costimulation (Signal 2). Reduced MHC II would impair initial T cell recognition, not necessarily proliferation if Signal 2 was present.
- (B) Impaired processing and presentation of viral antigens by APCs due to defects in proteasomal function: Similar to (A), this would affect Signal 1 (antigen presentation). If antigen presentation is severely impaired, T cell activation would be minimal. However, the patient *does* mount an IgM response, indicating that antigen presentation and B cell activation are occurring, and likely some T cell priming is also happening, albeit suboptimally. The specific finding of reduced CD28 points more directly to a costimulation defect.
- (D) Production of immunosuppressive cytokines, such as IL-10 and TGF-β, by regulatory T cells (Tregs) that inhibit T cell proliferation: Tregs do produce IL-10 and TGF-β to suppress immune responses. While Treg activity might be involved in controlling the immune response to Dengue, the primary defect highlighted by the reduced CD28 expression is a lack of costimulation, not necessarily active suppression by Tregs. Reduced CD28 is a direct defect in the T cell's ability to receive a critical activation signal.
- (E) Apoptosis of activated T cells induced by Fas-FasL interaction triggered by high levels of viral antigens: Fas-FasL interaction is a mechanism for regulating immune responses and inducing apoptosis, often in activated T cells. While this can occur during viral infections, it doesn't directly explain the *diminished proliferation* in the first place, especially when linked to the specific finding of reduced CD28 expression. Reduced CD28 points to a failure in the initial activation/proliferation step due to lack of costimulation.

#### High-Yield Comparison Table (核心考点与易混淆病原体/免疫缺陷横向对比):
| Feature                 | Correct Answer (C) - Reduced CD28/Costimulation | (A) Reduced MHC II | (B) Impaired Antigen Processing | (D) Treg Suppression | (E) Fas-FasL Apoptosis |
| :---------------------- | :---------------------------------------------- | :---------------- | :----------------------------- | :------------------ | :-------------------- |
| **Primary Defect**       | Lack of Costimulatory Signal (Signal 2)         | Deficient Signal 1 | Deficient Signal 1            | Active Suppression  | Cell Death            |
| **Affected Cell**        | T cell (CD28)                                   | APC (MHC II)      | APC (Proteasome)              | Treg                | Activated T cell      |
| **Impact on T Cell**     | Anergy, reduced proliferation                  | No initial activation | No initial activation         | Inhibition         | Cell death            |
| **Key Molecules**        | CD28, B7 (CD80/CD86)                            | MHC II            | Proteasome                    | IL-10, TGF-β        | Fas, FasL             |
| **Clinical Consequence** | Impaired adaptive immunity                     | Impaired adaptive immunity | Impaired adaptive immunity    | Immune regulation  | Immune regulation     |

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The vignette highlights the critical role of costimulatory signals (CD28-B7 interaction) in T cell activation. Lack of costimulation leads to T cell anergy or reduced proliferation, even if the T cell receptor is engaged. This is a key concept in understanding peripheral tolerance and T cell responses in various conditions, including viral infections and transplantation. Differentiate this from defects in antigen presentation (MHC downregulation, impaired processing) which affect Signal 1, or active suppression by Tregs or apoptosis mechanisms.
